```text
# Attribution, chronology and status

This record is part of the scientific evidence, not a substitute for running or understanding the code.

| Material | Origin / attribution | Status in this release |
|---|---|---|
| SCADA, nominal model, configuration | Vrachimis et al., original Zenodo record 4017659, CC-BY-4.0 | External data; exact bytes checked against official checksums |
| Existing models and published performance | Authors cited in the Milestone 2 bibliography / literature index | Literature-reported; their training was not reproduced |
| n1(+15), local signals and planned comparisons | Project proposal developed through discussion with the student and AI assistance | Proposed research; no claim of algorithmic invention or worldwide priority |
| Descriptive calculations and figures | Project-specific code generated/refined and executed with OpenAI Codex assistance on the project computer | Computationally executed; not represented as independently coded by the student |
| Historical literature collection/grouping | AI-assisted searching, reading and project-specific classification | Curated, incomplete inventory; automatic recount is reproducible |
| Portable reproduction package | Refactored after professor feedback for this public repository | New public packaging; not claimed to have been accessible when the report was submitted |
| Student's independent execution, understanding or sign-off | Requires an actual student action and an explicit record | Not established by an automated run; no signature or review is invented |

## What has actually been executed

The pipeline checks eight annual SCADA tables and recomputes Jan-Sep 2018 descriptive
statistics, persistence errors, correlations, daily profiles, nominal pipe paths and historical
literature counts. Each completed run records versions and SHA-256 hashes in
`results/reproduced/run_manifest.json`. That manifest supports a particular execution,
not a claim that a student independently designed or wrote every component.

## What remains proposed

Training Gradient Boosting, Ridge or Random Forest; assessing future-pressure prediction
on 2019; determining the incremental value of extra signals; and claiming an improvement
over persistence. None of these outcomes is implied by reproducing the EDA.

## Historical versus newly refactored code

`provenance/historical_scripts/*.py.txt` are unchanged snapshots from the earlier local
project. They retain historical paths, dependencies and report-editing side effects, so they
are **reading material only**. The historical figure bytes and their hashes are also archived.
Use `reproduce.py` and the `repro/` modules for an actual rerun.

The current script refactoring and new public links are post-feedback corrections. They must
not be described retroactively as evidence that was provided in the original submission.

## Plots and quantitative originality

The daily overlays and correlation cells are computed from the released measurements.
The requested daily-overlay presentation was inspired by an illustrative plot supplied during
the project discussion; no other paper's pressure curves or correlation values were copied.
Figure 1 is an interpretive supply schematic, not a new hydraulic simulation. Its original
drawing source and nominal-model inputs are identified in the evidence ledger.

Numerical calculations made from public data are not, by themselves, a new learning method.
Research novelty, correct numerical reproduction and authorship are separate questions.
```

### aggregated_output

````text
# DS675 Mini-Project: reproducible BattLeDIM / L-Town evidence

Public supporting material for **Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting**, NJIT DS675.
The proposed task is recorded pressure at **n1, 15 minutes ahead**. This release addresses
the provenance questions raised after Milestone 2. **It reproduces descriptive analyses;
it does not train or evaluate the proposed learning models.**

## Start here

| Need | Open |
|---|---|
| Which claim or figure is supported by which code? | [Claim-to-evidence ledger](docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE.md) |
| Who produced what, and with what assistance? | [Attribution and analysis status](docs/ATTRIBUTION.md) |
| Inspect the calculations step by step | [Executed explanatory notebook](notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb) |
| See the recomputed numerical results | [EDA statistics](results/reproduced/eda_statistics.json), [verification](results/reproduced/verification.json) |
| Inspect Figure 4's complete underlying inventory | [47 source records](literature/inventory_sources.csv), [35 group assignments](literature/group_assignments.csv) |
| Read the papers and their redistribution status | [Literature index](literature/README.md) |
| Understand and run the material step by step | [Reproduction guide](docs/REPRODUCTION_GUIDE.md) |

## Reproduce from a fresh clone

Python **3.14** is the tested local environment. The pinned scientific dependencies are in
`requirements.txt`. Raw data are downloaded from the fixed original Zenodo record;
no institutional login is needed for those data.

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Andres-ArangoMartinez/DS675_MiniProject.git
cd DS675_MiniProject
python -m venv .venv
```

Select the environment's Python. On Windows PowerShell:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe reproduce.py --download
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

On Linux/macOS, use `.venv/bin/python` in the same commands. Do not run the historical
source snapshots in `provenance/historical_scripts/`; they document the earlier work,
whereas `reproduce.py` is the portable entry point.

The first run downloads approximately 170 MB. Each of the 11 required files is verified
against its official MD5, then recorded with SHA-256. The CSVs are not duplicated in Git:
[data/source_manifest.json](data/source_manifest.json) gives the exact source URLs,
checksums, attribution and **CC-BY-4.0** license. A local flat cache can instead be supplied
with `--import-data /path/to/cache`. An altered file fails validation.

## Expected results

| Quantity | Recomputed value |
|---|---:|
| EDA period | January-September 2018 |
| Timestamps / complete days | 78,624 / 273 |
| Valid n1(+15) pairs | 78,621 |
| Persistence MAE | 0.05177624298851451 m |
| Persistence RMSE | 0.06849175638676988 m |
| n1 pressure range | 25.67-29.36 m |
| Nominal T1-to-n1 / n4 / n31 pipe distances | 891.3208 / 573.7565 / 307.7926 m |
| Reported rounded distances | 891 / 574 / 308 m |
| Historical literature inventory | 47 source records, 35 related-work groups |

These are **2018 descriptive results**, not final 2019 forecast scores. The pipeline reads
2019 only to check table structure, timestamps and missing values. It never fits a learned
model or computes a 2019 prediction score.

Outputs include the eight-table audit, correlations, sample forecast rows, full derived
tables (generated locally, ignored by Git), nominal pipe paths, regrouped literature counts,
Figures 2-4, package versions and input/code/output hashes. See
[results/reproduced/](results/reproduced/). The portable plots use DejaVu Serif; archived
report images retain their original bytes. Numerical reproduction does not imply pixel identity.

## Scope and limits

- This package was assembled and executed **with OpenAI Codex assistance**. It does not
  establish independent student implementation, understanding or review. See the attribution record.
- The 35-group inventory is a **historical, AI-assisted curated collection**, not an exhaustive
  systematic review. Recounting it does not repeat a historical search engine or validate every judgment.
- Ahmed et al. (2025) is a newly identified related study, **outside the historical 35-group count**.
  Its five signals and reconstruction objective must be considered in the next literature revision.
- Pipe distance is not pressure-propagation delay. A tank boundary does not prove upstream
  statistical independence. A fixed nominal PRV setting does not fix every remote pressure.
- Gradient Boosting, Ridge, Random Forest and the A/B/C feature comparisons remain **proposed**.
- The previous report is preserved as a historical artifact; this release does not silently
  rewrite the submitted report or claim that these public links existed at submission time.

## Sources and third-party rights

Data: Vrachimis et al., [Dataset of BattLeDIM, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.4017659](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.4017659).
Benchmark: [Vrachimis et al. (2022)](https://doi.org/10.1061/(ASCE)WR.1943-5452.0001601).
PDFs are included only where a document or source record supplies a redistribution license.
Other works have original DOI/publisher/author links in the literature catalog. Public access
or an institutional download alone is not treated as redistribution permission.
Third-party papers keep their own licenses; no repository-wide license is applied to them.

All paths below are inside this public repository. Run `python reproduce.py --download`.
The exact source release and MD5 values are in [the data manifest](../data/source_manifest.json).

Not regenerated by the portable pipeline |
| Figure 4: 35 groups, categories 27/3/2/2/1, six selected comparisons | `literature/inventory_sources.csv` + `group_assignments.csv` | [`recount`](../repro/screening.py), [`screening`](../repro/figures.py) | [results/reproduced/screening_summary.json](../results/reproduced/screening_summary.json), `figures/figure4_historical_screening.png` | Historical AI-assisted curated collection; 47 source records, not 47 independent studies; not exhaustive |
| Previous authors' model descriptions and scores | Original articles / author code linked in the literature index | Source reading; no published model retraining | Literature catalog and linked original sources | Literature-reported, distinct from project computations |
| Forecast n1(+15) with learned models and compare extra inputs | Planned past-only data preparation and chronological periods | Not trained in this release | No prediction-score output is claimed | Proposed work; model performance and feature value remain unknown |

## Numerical audit and rendering audit are different

`verification.json` compares freshly recomputed values against the historical values
in `provenance/expected_report_values.json`. `run_manifest.json` records exact bytes, code,
packages and output hashes. Historical report figures are preserved in
`provenance/figures_as_reported/`. The regenerated plots use the same data and calculations;
they are not advertised as pixel-identical copies.

## Screening boundaries

Recreating Figure 4 means calculating counts from the complete published inventory. It does
not mean rerunning a 2026 search engine or proving that every relevant work was found.
Read [the screening protocol](../literature/SEARCH_AND_SCREENING.md), especially its
historical omission of Ahmed et al. (2025).

# Historical search, screening and grouping

## What is reproducible

The committed CSVs reproduce **Figure 4 of report v0.30**: 47 source records assigned to
35 related-work groups, with categories 27 leak/localization, 3 current-state estimation,
2 forecasting, 2 anomaly/fault detection and 1 sensor placement. Six groups were selected
for detailed discussion. Papers, versions and author repositories describing one method
are grouped rather than counted independently.

`inventory_sources.csv` retains every source ID, original URL, evidence locator and saved
checksum/commit from the historical inventory. `group_assignments.csv` makes all group
IDs, categories and selected/not-selected decisions visible. `repro/screening.py` joins the
files and recomputes counts. The code does not contain the category totals as its counting rule.

## Historical collection strategy

The earlier local search record was an exploratory, AI-assisted literature collection,
not a systematic review or a Scopus/Web of Science bibliometric study. It started from
the BattLeDIM committee, official dataset, benchmark paper and competition reports;
followed citations and author code; then searched for pressure prediction/reconstruction,
graphs, autoencoders, dictionaries, ensembles and related evaluations.

Representative queries retained from that earlier search record include:

- `BattLeDIM L-Town leak detection localization github benchmark`
- `L-Town pressure prediction forecasting github BattLeDIM`
- `"BattLeDIM" "pressure forecasting"`
- `"L-Town" "pressure" "forecasting" learning`
- `"BattLeDIM" "15-minute" forecasting`
- `"BattLeDIM" "github" "autoencoder"`
- `"SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning" pdf`

The counted set retains original or explicitly modified L-Town studies. Other networks,
general reviews and tools are not in the historical denominator. A method's primary goal
determines its category even if pressure forecasting is an intermediate diagnostic step.
This is a scholarly classification, not a result established by counting code.

## Limits and post-feedback addition

Search engines and publisher availability change; a complete historical result log was
not retained. The collection and classifications are not independently human-validated
merely because the CSV joins pass. The inventory does not establish worldwide priority.

**Ahmed et al. (2025), DOI 10.15131/shef.data.29921204.v1, was missing from the historical
inventory.** Its PDF/link is listed as a post-feedback addition in the literature index; it
is not silently inserted into the 35-group historical count. A future literature revision
must reassess grouping and selection in light of that paper.

name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
````

Not regenerated by the portable pipeline |\\r\\n| Figure 4: 35 groups, categories 27/3/2/2/1, six selected comparisons | `literature/inventory_sources.csv` + `group_assignments.csv` | [`recount`](../repro/screening.py), [`screening`](../repro/figures.py) | [results/reproduced/screening_summary.json](../results/reproduced/screening_summary.json), `figures/figure4_historical_screening.png` | Historical AI-assisted curated collection; 47 source records, not 47 independent studies; not exhaustive |\\r\\n| Previous authors' model descriptions and scores | Original articles / author code linked in the literature index | Source reading; no published model retraining | Literature catalog and linked original sources | Literature-reported, distinct from project computations |\\r\\n| Forecast n1(+15) with learned models and compare extra inputs | Planned past-only data preparation and chronological periods | Not trained in this release | No prediction-score output is claimed | Proposed work; model performance and feature value remain unknown |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Numerical audit and rendering audit are different\\r\\n\\r\\n`verification.json` compares freshly recomputed values against the historical values\\r\\nin `provenance/expected_report_values.json`. `run_manifest.json` records exact bytes, code,\\r\\npackages and output hashes. Historical report figures are preserved in\\r\\n`provenance/figures_as_reported/`. The regenerated plots use the same data and calculations;\\r\\nthey are not advertised as pixel-identical copies.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Screening boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\nRecreating Figure 4 means calculating counts from the complete published inventory. It does\\r\\nnot mean rerunning a 2026 search engine or proving that every relevant work was found.\\r\\nRead [the screening protocol](../literature/SEARCH_AND_SCREENING.md), especially its\\r\\nhistorical omission of Ahmed et al. (2025).\\r\\n\\r\\n# Historical search, screening and grouping\\r\\n\\r\\n## What is reproducible\\r\\n\\r\\nThe committed CSVs reproduce **Figure 4 of report v0.30**: 47 source records assigned to\\r\\n35 related-work groups, with categories 27 leak/localization, 3 current-state estimation,\\r\\n2 forecasting, 2 anomaly/fault detection and 1 sensor placement. Six groups were selected\\r\\nfor detailed discussion. Papers, versions and author repositories describing one method\\r\\nare grouped rather than counted independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n`inventory_sources.csv` retains every source ID, original URL, evidence locator and saved\\r\\nchecksum/commit from the historical inventory. `group_assignments.csv` makes all group\\r\\nIDs, categories and selected/not-selected decisions visible. `repro/screening.py` joins the\\r\\nfiles and recomputes counts. The code does not contain the category totals as its counting rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Historical collection strategy\\r\\n\\r\\nThe earlier local search record was an exploratory, AI-assisted literature collection,\\r\\nnot a systematic review or a Scopus/Web of Science bibliometric study. It started from\\r\\nthe BattLeDIM committee, official dataset, benchmark paper and competition reports;\\r\\nfollowed citations and author code; then searched for pressure prediction/reconstruction,\\r\\ngraphs, autoencoders, dictionaries, ensembles and related evaluations.\\r\\n\\r\\nRepresentative queries retained from that earlier search record include:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `BattLeDIM L-Town leak detection localization github benchmark`\\r\\n- `L-Town pressure prediction forecasting github BattLeDIM`\\r\\n- `\\\"BattLeDIM\\\" \\\"pressure forecasting\\\"`\\r\\n- `\\\"L-Town\\\" \\\"pressure\\\" \\\"forecasting\\\" learning`\\r\\n- `\\\"BattLeDIM\\\" \\\"15-minute\\\" forecasting`\\r\\n- `\\\"BattLeDIM\\\" \\\"github\\\" \\\"autoencoder\\\"`\\r\\n- `\\\"SAM-kNN Regressor for Online Learning\\\" pdf`\\r\\n\\r\\nThe counted set retains original or explicitly modified L-Town studies. Other networks,\\r\\ngeneral reviews and tools are not in the historical denominator. A method's primary goal\\r\\ndetermines its category even if pressure forecasting is an intermediate diagnostic step.\\r\\nThis is a scholarly classification, not a result established by counting code.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Limits and post-feedback addition\\r\\n\\r\\nSearch engines and publisher availability change; a complete historical result log was\\r\\nnot retained. The collection and classifications are not independently human-validated\\r\\nmerely because the CSV joins pass. The inventory does not establish worldwide priority.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Ahmed et al. (2025), DOI 10.15131/shef.data.29921204.v1, was missing from the historical\\r\\ninventory.** Its PDF/link is listed as a post-feedback addition in the literature index; it\\r\\nis not silently inserted into the 35-group historical count. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnode container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.\\r\\n   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.\\r\\n   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.\\r\\n2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.\\r\\n3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.\\r\\n4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Fill And Validate AcroForms\\r\\n\\r\\nVisual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not fla…3772 tokens truncated…isturbances. Although closed-loop field implementation is beyond the scope of this study, the framework is explicitly conceived as a preparatory step for real-time deployment. The main conclusion is that a transparent, data-driven methodology grounded in field measurements can reduce modeling and tuning effort while yielding controller designs compatible with standard programmable logic controller (PLC) and supervisory control and data acquisition (SCADA) platforms, enabling utilities to progress systematically toward practical real-time pressure control.\\n\\n## Practical Applications\\n\\nThis study offers practical guidance for water utilities seeking to improve pressure management while reducing water losses and infrastructure wear. Using real operational data, the proposed approach shows how effective pressure regulation strategies can be derived directly from field measurements and implemented with standard PLC and SCADA systems already used by most utilities. As a result, the methodology is accessible without requiring advanced hydraulic-modeling expertise or specialized simulation tools. Pressure management is particularly important because daily demand variations often produce excessive pressures during low consumption periods (e.g., nighttime). These pressure surges increase the likelihood of pipe bursts, background leakage, and premature deterioration of network assets. The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\\n\\n\\n## Data Availability Statement\\n\\nSome or all data, models, or code that support the findings of this study are available from the corresponding author upon reasonable request.\\n\\n## Acknowledgments\\n\\nThe authors would like to thank Aquaoccidente S.A. # Entregables del MiniProject

```text
El usuario exige que todos los entregables conserven el mismo formato visual.

- La referencia es `Milestone 1/Milestone1.tex` y su PDF evaluado.
- Consultar `Plantilla/ESTANDAR_VISUAL.md` y usar `Plantilla/milestone_visual.tex` para los informes posteriores.
- Conservar tipografÃ­a, mÃ¡rgenes, paleta, encabezados, tablas y recuadros; cambiar solo contenido, metadatos y estado de la etapa.
- Separar contenido y formato. No introducir un diseÃ±o alternativo al generar un nuevo milestone o versiÃ³n.
- Conservar originales y versiones anteriores. Un borrador no se marca como entregado o aprobado sin evidencia.
- Antes de publicar/entregar, compilar y revisar visualmente todas las pÃ¡ginas; comprobar tablas, desbordamientos, referencias y longitud requerida.
"""Checks, past/future alignment, descriptive EDA and nominal pipe-graph distances."""
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path
import heapq
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import yaml

SIGNALS = ['n1', 'n4', 'n31', 'T1', 'PUMP_1', 'AMR_n1']
TARGET = 'n1_plus15'
PERIOD_START = '2018-01-01'
PERIOD_END = '2018-09-30'


def read_and_check_tables(raw):
    """2019 is read for structural checks only; no 2019 prediction score is computed."""
    raw = Path(raw)
    structural = []
    training = {}
    for year in [2018, 2019]:
        previous_index = None
        for kind, columns in [('Pressures', 33), ('Flows', 3), ('Levels', 1), ('Demands', 82)]:
            name = f'{year}_SCADA_{kind}.csv'
            frame = pd.read_csv(raw / name, sep=';', decimal=',', index_col=0, parse_dates=True)
            if frame.shape != (105120, columns):
                raise ValueError(f'Unexpected shape: {name}: {frame.shape}')
            assert frame.index.is_unique and frame.index.is_monotonic_increasing
            assert frame.index.to_series().diff().dropna().eq(pd.Timedelta(minutes=5)).all()
            assert not frame.isna().any().any()
            assert frame.index[0] == pd.Timestamp(f'{year}-01-01 00:00')
            assert frame.index[-1] == pd.Timestamp(f'{year}-12-31 23:55')
            if previous_index is not None:
                assert frame.index.equals(previous_index), f'Timestamp mismatch: {name}'
            previous_index = frame.index
            structural.append({
                'filename': name, 'year': year, 'records': len(frame), 'features': columns,
                'duplicate_timestamps': 0, 'missing_values': 0, 'spacing_minutes': 5,
                'timestamps_match_other_tables_in_year': True,
                'use': 'structure_and_Jan_Sep_EDA' if year == 2018 else 'structure_only',
            })
            if year == 2018:
                training[kind] = frame.loc[PERIOD_START:PERIOD_END].copy()
            print(f'Checked {name}: {len(frame):,} rows, {columns} channels', flush=True)
    pressures = training['Pressures']
    x = pressures[['n1', 'n4', 'n31']].join(training['Levels'][['T1']])
    x = x.join(training['Flows'][['PUMP_1']])
    x = x.join(training['Demands'][['n1']].rename(columns={'n1': 'AMR_n1'}))
    assert list(x.columns) == SIGNALS and x.shape == (78624, 6)
    return x, structural


def make_forecast_pairs(x, steps=3):
    """Crop the period first, then shift: the final 3 labels cannot cross September."""
    if steps < 1:
        raise ValueError('The forecast lead must be positive.')
    assert x.index.is_unique and x.index.is_monotonic_increasing
    pairs = x.copy()
    pairs[TARGET] = x['n1'].shift(-steps)
    pairs = pairs.dropna()
    return pairs


def describe(x):
    pairs = make_forecast_pairs(x)
    error = pairs[TARGET] - pairs['n1']
    corr = pairs.corr(method='pearson')
    statistics = {
        'period_start': PERIOD_START, 'period_end': PERIOD_END, 'days': 273,
        'rows': len(x), 'forecast_pairs': len(pairs), 'lead_minutes': 15,
        'n1_range_m': [float(x.n1.min()), float(x.n1.max())],
        'persistence_MAE_m': float(error.abs().mean()),
        'persistence_RMSE_m': float(np.sqrt((error ** 2).mean())),
        'correlation_with_future_n1': {name: float(corr.loc[name, TARGET]) for name in SIGNALS},
        'signals': SIGNALS, 'target': TARGET,
        'interpretation': 'Descriptive Jan-Sep 2018 results; not a final 2019 test score.',
        'learned_models_fitted': False,
    }
    assert len(pairs) == 78621
    return statistics, corr, pairs


def parse_inp(path):
    sections = {}
    section = None
    for line in Path(path).read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig').splitlines():
        text = line.split(';', 1)[0].strip()
        if text.startswith('['):
            section = text.upper()
            sections[section] = []
        elif text and section:
            sections[section].append(text.split())
    return sections


def nominal_topology(raw):
    """Undirected, initially open pipe graph; excludes pumps and PRVs as boundaries."""
    raw = Path(raw)
    sections = parse_inp(raw / 'L-TOWN.inp')
    config = yaml.safe_load((raw / 'dataset_configuration.yaml').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    graph = defaultdict(list)
    closed = []
    for row in sections['[PIPES]']:
        if len(row) > 7 and row[7].upper() == 'CLOSED':
            closed.append(row[0])
            continue
        left, right, length = row[1], row[2], float(row[3])
        graph[left].append((right, length, row[0]))
        graph[right].append((left, length, row[0]))

    def component(root):
        seen = set()
        stack = [root]
        while stack:
            node = stack.pop()
            if node in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(node)
            stack.extend(peer for peer, _, _ in graph[node] if peer not in seen)
        return seen

    distances = {}
    paths = {}
    queue = [(0.0, 'T1', ())]
    while queue:
        distance, node, pipes = heapq.heappop(queue)
        if node in distances:
            continue
        distances[node] = distance
        paths[node] = list(pipes)
        for peer, length, pipe in graph[node]:
            if peer not in distances:
                heapq.heappush(queue, (distance + length, peer, pipes + (pipe,)))

    junctions = {row[0] for row in sections['[JUNCTIONS]']}
    sectors = {}
    components = {}
    for area, root in [('A', 'n300'), ('B', 'n226'), ('C', 'T1')]:
        nodes = component(root)
        components[area] = nodes
        sensors = [node for node in config['pressure_sensors'] if node in nodes]
        sectors[area] = {
            'junctions': len(nodes & junctions), 'pressure_sensors': sensors,
            'pressure_sensor_count': len(sensors), 'AMR_count': len(set(config['amrs']) & nodes),
        }
    assert not any(components[a] & components[b] for a, b in [('A', 'B'), ('A', 'C'), ('B', 'C')])
    upstream = sorted(junctions - set.union(*components.values()))
    assert sum(s['junctions'] for s in sectors.values()) + len(upstream) == 782
    measured_distances = {node: {'distance_m': distances[node], 'pipe_path': paths[node]} for node in ['n1', 'n4', 'n31']}
    return {
        'model': 'L-TOWN.inp (nominal)', 'algorithm': 'Dijkstra shortest total pipe length from T1',
        'scope': 'Initially open undirected pipes; pumps and PRVs excluded from sector connections.',
        'network_counts': {name: len(sections[key]) for name, key in [
            ('junctions', '[JUNCTIONS]'), ('reservoirs', '[RESERVOIRS]'), ('tanks', '[TANKS]'),
            ('pipes', '[PIPES]'), ('pumps', '[PUMPS]'), ('valves', '[VALVES]')]},
        'sectors': sectors, 'upstream_boundary_junctions': upstream, 'closed_pipes_excluded': closed,
        'T1_to_measured_pressure_nodes': measured_distances,
        'PRVs': [{'id': r[0], 'from': r[1], 'to': r[2], 'type': r[4], 'nominal_setting_m': float(r[5])} for r in sections['[VALVES]']],
        'nominal_controls': [' '.join(row) for row in sections['[CONTROLS]']],
        'feature_filter_counts': {'all_channels': 119, 'Area_C_and_pump_candidates': 87, 'selected_signals': 6},
        'limitations': [
            'Pipe-path length is not hydraulic sensitivity, pressure propagation delay or travel time.',
            'Nominal setpoints do not establish observed time-varying valve states.',
            'A tank supply boundary does not prove statistical independence from upstream conditions.',
            'PUMP_1 is tank inflow; AMR_n1 is nodal consumption, not sector inflow.',
        ],
    }

```

= '2018-01-01'\\r\\nPERIOD_END = '2018-09-30'\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef read_and_check_tables(raw):\\r\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"2019 is read for structural checks only; no 2019 prediction score is computed.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n    raw = Path(raw)\\r\\n    structural = []\\r\\n    training = {}\\r\\n    for year in [2018, 2019]:\\r\\n        previous_index = None\\r\\n        for kind, columns in [('Pressures', 33), ('Flows', 3), ('Levels', 1), ('Demands', 82)]:\\r\\n            name = f'{year}_SCADA_{kind}.csv'\\r\\n            frame = pd.read_csv(raw / name, sep=';', decimal=',', index_col=0, parse_dates=True)\\r\\n            if frame.shape != (105120, columns):\\r\\n                raise ValueError(f'Unexpected shape: {name}: {frame.shape}')\\r\\n            assert frame.index.is_unique and frame.index.is_monotonic_increasing\\r\\n            assert frame.index.to_series().diff().dropna().eq(pd.Timedelta(minutes=5)).all()\\r\\n            assert not frame.isna().any().any()\\r\\n            assert frame.index[0] == pd.Timestamp(f'{year}-01-01 00:00')\\r\\n            assert frame.index[-1] == pd.Timestamp(f'{year}-12-31 23:55')\\r\\n            if previous_index is not None:\\r\\n                assert frame.index.equals(previous_index), f'Timestamp mismatch: {name}'\\r\\n            previous_index = frame.index\\r\\n            structural.append({\\r\\n                'filename': name, 'year': year, 'records': len(frame), 'features': columns,\\r\\n                'duplicate_timestamps': 0, 'missing_values': 0, 'spacing_minutes': 5,\\r\\n                'timestamps_match_other_tables_in_year': True,\\r\\n                'use': 'structure_and_Jan_Sep_EDA' if year == 2018 else 'structure_only',\\r\\n            })\\r\\n            if year == 2018:\\r\\n                training[kind] = frame.loc[PERIOD_START:PERIOD_END].copy()\\r\\n            print(f'Checked {name}: {len(frame):,} rows, {columns} channels', flush=True)\\r\\n    pressures = training['Pressures']\\r\\n    x = pressures[['n1', 'n4', 'n31']].join(training['Levels'][['T1']])\\r\\n    x = x.join(training['Flows'][['PUMP_1']])\\r\\n    x = x.join(training['Demands'][['n1']].rename(columns={'n1': 'AMR_n1'}))\\r\\n    assert list(x.columns) == SIGNALS and x.shape == (78624, 6)\\r\\n    return x, structural\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef make_forecast_pairs(x, steps=3):\\r\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Crop the period first, then shift: the final 3 labels cannot cross September.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n    if steps < 1:\\r\\n        raise ValueError('The forecast lead must be positive.')\\r\\n    assert x.index.is_unique and x.index.is_monotonic_increasing\\r\\n    pairs = x.copy()\\r\\n    pairs[TARGET] = x['n1'].shift(-steps)\\r\\n    pairs = pairs.dropna()\\r\\n    return pairs\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef describe(x):\\r\\n    pairs = make_forecast_pairs(x)\\r\\n    error = pairs[TARGET] - pairs['n1']\\r\\n    corr = pairs.corr(method='pearson')\\r\\n    statistics = {\\r\\n        'period_start': PERIOD_START, 'period_end': PERIOD_END, 'days': 273,\\r\\n        'rows': len(x), 'forecast_pairs': len(pairs), float(np.sqrt((error ** 2).mean())),\\r\\n        'correlation_with_future_n1': {name: float(corr.loc[name, TARGET]) for name in SIGNALS},\\r\\n        'signals': SIGNALS, 'target': TARGET,\\r\\n        'interpretation': 'Descriptive Jan-Sep 2018 results; not a final 2019 test score.',\\r\\n        'learned_models_fitted': False,\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    assert len(pairs) == 78621\\r\\n    return statistics, corr, pairs\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef parse_inp(path):\\r\\n    sections = {}\\r\\n    section = None\\r\\n    for line in Path(path).read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig').splitlines():\\r\\n        text = line.split(';', 1)[0].strip()\\r\\n        if text.startswith('['):\\r\\n            section = text.upper()\\r\\n            sections[section] = []\\r\\n        elif text and section:\\r\\n            sections[section].append(text.split())\\r\\n    return sections\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\ndef nominal_topology(raw):\\r\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Undirected, initially open pipe graph; excludes pumps and PRVs as boundaries.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\r\\n    raw = Path(raw)\\r\\n    sections = parse_inp(raw / 'L-TOWN.inp')\\r\\n    config = yaml.safe_load((raw / 'dataset_configuration.yaml').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\\r\\n    graph = defaultdict(list)\\r\\n    closed = []\\r\\n    for row in sections['[PIPES]']:\\r\\n        if len(row) > 7 and row[7].upper() == 'CLOSED':\\r\\n            closed.append(row[0])\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n        left, right, length = row[1], row[2], float(row[3])\\r\\n        graph[left].append((right, length, row[0]))\\r\\n        graph[right].append((left, length, row[0]))\\r\\n\\r\\n    def component(root):\\r\\n        seen = set()\\r\\n        stack = [root]\\r\\n        while stack:\\r\\n            node = stack.pop()\\r\\n            if node in seen:\\r\\n                continue\\r\\n            seen.add(node)\\r\\n            stack.extend(peer for peer, _, _ in graph[node] if peer not in seen)\\r\\n        return seen\\r\\n\\r\\n    distances = {}\\r\\n    paths = {}\\r\\n    queue = [(0.0, 'T1', ())]\\r\\n    while queue:\\r\\n        distance, node, pipes = heapq.heappop(queue)\\r\\n        if node in distances:\\r\\n            continue\\r\\n        distances[node] = distance\\r\\n        paths[node] = list(pipes)\\r\\n        for peer, length, pipe in graph[node]:\\r\\n            if peer not in distances:\\r\\n                heapq.heappush(queue, (distance + length, peer, pipes + (pipe,)))\\r\\n\\r\\n    junctions = {row[0] for row in sections['[JUNCTIONS]']}\\r\\n    sectors = {}\\r\\n    components = {}\\r\\n    for area, root in [('A', 'n300'), ('B', 'n226'), ('C', 'T1')]:\\r\\n        nodes = component(root)\\r\\n        components[area] = nodes\\r\\n        sensors = [node for node in config['pressure_sensors'] if node in nodes]\\r\\n        sectors[area] = {\\r\\n            'junctions': len(nodes & junctions), 'pressure_sensors': sensors,\\r\\n            'pressure_sensor_count': len(sensors), 'AMR_count': len(set(config['amrs']) & nodes),\\r\\n        }\\r\\n    assert not any(components[a] & components[b] for a, b in [('A', 'B'), ('A', 'C'), ('B', 'C')])\\r\\n    upstream = sorted(junctions - set.union(*components.values()))\\r\\n    assert sum(s['junctions'] for s in sectors.values()) + len(upstream) == 782\\r\\n    measured_distances = {node: {'distance_m': distances[node], 'pipe_path': paths[node]} for node in ['n1', 'n4', 'n31']}\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n        'model': 'L-TOWN.inp (nominal)', 'algorithm': 'Dijkstra shortest total pipe length from T1',\\r\\n        'scope': 'Initially open undirected pipes; pumps and PRVs excluded from sector connections.',\\r\\n        'network_counts': {name: len(sections[key]) for name, key in [\\r\\n            ('junctions', '[JUNCTIONS]'), ('reservoirs', '[RESERVOIRS]'), ('tanks', '[TANKS]'),\\r\\n            ('pipes', '[PIPES]'), ('pumps', '[PUMPS]'), ('valves', '[VALVES]')]},\\r\\n        'sectors': sectors, 'upstream_boundary_junctions': upstream, 'closed_pipes_excluded': closed,\\r\\n        'T1_to_measured_pressure_nodes': measured_distances,\\r\\n        'PRVs': [{'id': r[0], 'from': r[1], 'to': r[2], 'type': r[4], 'nominal_setting_m': float(r[5])} for r in sections['[VALVES]']],\\r\\n        'nominal_controls': [' '.join(row) for row in sections['[CONTROLS]']],\\r\\n        'feature_filter_counts': {'all_channels': 119, 'Area_C_and_pump_candidates': 87, 'selected_signals': 6},\\r\\n        'limitations': # Attribution, chronology and status

````text
This record is part of the scientific evidence, not a substitute for running or understanding the code.

| Material | Origin / attribution | Status in this release |
|---|---|---|
| SCADA, nominal model, configuration | Vrachimis et al., original Zenodo record 4017659, CC-BY-4.0 | External data; exact bytes checked against official checksums |
| Existing models and published performance | Authors cited in the Milestone 2 bibliography / literature index | Literature-reported; their training was not reproduced |
| n1(+15), local signals and planned comparisons | Project proposal developed through discussion with the student and AI assistance | Proposed research; no claim of algorithmic invention or worldwide priority |
| Descriptive calculations and figures | Project-specific code generated/refined and executed with OpenAI Codex assistance on the project computer | Computationally executed; not represented as independently coded by the student |
| Historical literature collection/grouping | AI-assisted searching, reading and project-specific classification | Curated, incomplete inventory; automatic recount is reproducible |
| Portable reproduction package | Refactored after professor feedback for this public repository | New public packaging; not claimed to have been accessible when the report was submitted |
| Student's independent execution, understanding or sign-off | Requires an actual student action and an explicit record | Not established by an automated run; no signature or review is invented |

## What has actually been executed

The pipeline checks eight annual SCADA tables and recomputes Jan-Sep 2018 descriptive
statistics, persistence errors, correlations, daily profiles, nominal pipe paths and historical
literature counts. Each completed run records versions and SHA-256 hashes in
`results/reproduced/run_manifest.json`. That manifest supports a particular execution,
not a claim that a student independently designed or wrote every component.

## What remains proposed

Training Gradient Boosting, Ridge or Random Forest; assessing future-pressure prediction
on 2019; determining the incremental value of extra signals; and claiming an improvement
over persistence. None of these outcomes is implied by reproducing the EDA.

## Historical versus newly refactored code

`provenance/historical_scripts/*.py.txt` are unchanged snapshots from the earlier local
project. They retain historical paths, dependencies and report-editing side effects, so they
are **reading material only**. The historical figure bytes and their hashes are also archived.
Use `reproduce.py` and the `repro/` modules for an actual rerun.

The current script refactoring and new public links are post-feedback corrections. They must
not be described retroactively as evidence that was provided in the original submission.

## Plots and quantitative originality

The daily overlays and correlation cells are computed from the released measurements.
The requested daily-overlay presentation was inspired by an illustrative plot supplied during
the project discussion; no other paper's pressure curves or correlation values were copied.
Figure 1 is an interpretive supply schematic, not a new hydraulic simulation. Its original
drawing source and nominal-model inputs are identified in the evidence ledger.

Numerical calculations made from public data are not, by themselves, a new learning method.
Research novelty, correct numerical reproduction and authorship are separate questions.

# How to verify the Milestone 2 work

This repository follows the chain **data -> code -> calculation -> figure -> claim**.
Preparation used OpenAI Codex assistance; it does not claim that the student independently
implemented or validated every step.

## 1. Run the analysis

Clone the repository with GitHub Desktop and open its folder. With Python 3.14 installed:

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe reproduce.py --download
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

The command downloads the required files from the original Zenodo record 4017659.
It checks MD5 values and also records SHA-256 hashes. Institutional credentials are not
needed. Large raw inputs remain in the Git-ignored `data/raw/` folder; small outputs and
figures are published for convenient inspection.

## 2. Read the outputs

- `table_checks.json`: rows, columns, missing values and continuity of the eight tables.
- `eda_statistics.json`: pressure range, persistence and Jan-Sep 2018 correlations.
- `correlations.csv`: the six inputs and the conceptually separate future label.
- `forecast_pairs_sample.csv`: example input rows and their n1 pressure 15 minutes later.
- `topology_audit.json`: sectors, sensors, exact distances and the pipes in each path.
- `screening_summary.json`: counts calculated from the complete historical inventory.
- `verification.json`: comparison of recomputed values with the report's claims.
- `run_manifest.json`: versions, provenance and hashes for that execution.

## 3. Understand the main questions

1. Why does shifting n1 by three rows represent +15 minutes?
2. Why are the last three September origins excluded?
3. Why is future pressure a label rather than an input?
4. What does a persistence MAE of about 5.18 cm mean for this period?
5. Why does high correlation not prove that an input improves a forecast?
6. Why is a pipe length of 891.3208 m not a pressure-propagation time?
7. Why are 47 source records grouped into 35 groups instead of counted as independent studies?

The notebook `notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb` walks through these operations.
Install `requirements-notebook.txt`, open it in an editor with Jupyter support, and select
the Python interpreter from `.venv`. For an automated execution with that environment:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/execute_notebook.py
```

## 4. Record an actual human review

Automatic output demonstrates computational execution, not individual understanding.
You can record the date of your own run, the commit used, outputs compared, concepts
understood and unresolved questions. No signature or human-review record is invented.

## What has NOT been done here

Gradient Boosting, Ridge and Random Forest have not been trained; their performance on
2019 has not been measured; and auxiliary-signal value has not been established. Published
model training was not reproduced either. Retain these boundaries when using this evidence
in a response to the professor or in the next report.

"""Public entry point: reproduce Milestone 2 descriptive evidence, not model training."""
from repro.pipeline import main

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

"""Single reproducible entry point for the feedback's computational evidence requests."""
import argparse
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import importlib.metadata
import json
from pathlib import Path
import platform
import sys
import numpy as np
from . import analysis, figures, screening
from .data import ROOT, digest, ensure_data


def dump(path, value):
    path.write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)+'\n', encoding='utf-8')


def run(data_dir, output_dir, download=False, import_data=None):
    output = Path(output_dir)
    output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    figure_dir = output / 'figures'
    figure_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    inputs = ensure_data(data_dir, download=download, import_folder=import_data)
    x, structure = analysis.read_and_check_tables(data_dir)
    stats, corr, pairs = analysis.describe(x)
    topology = analysis.nominal_topology(data_dir)
    counts = screening.recount()
    dump(output / 'table_checks.json', structure)
    dump(output / 'eda_statistics.json', stats)
    dump(output / 'topology_audit.json', topology)
    dump(output / 'screening_summary.json', counts)
    corr.to_csv(output / 'correlations.csv', index_label='variable')
    x.head(20).to_csv(output / 'selected_inputs_sample.csv', index_label='forecast_origin')
    pairs.head(20).to_csv(output / 'forecast_pairs_sample.csv', index_label='forecast_origin')
    x.to_csv(output / 'selected_inputs_full.csv.gz', index_label='forecast_origin', compression='gzip')
    pairs.to_csv(output / 'forecast_pairs_full.csv.gz', index_label='forecast_origin', compression='gzip')
    figures.daily_profiles(x, figure_dir)
    figures.correlations(corr, figure_dir)
    figures.screening(counts, figure_dir)

    # Expected values are fixed historical report claims, independent of this refactor.
    expected = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/expected_report_values.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    matches = []
    for key in ['rows', 'forecast_pairs', 'days']:
        assert stats[key] == expected[key], (key, stats[key], expected[key])
        matches.append(key)
    for key in ['persistence_MAE_m', 'persistence_RMSE_m']:
        assert abs(stats[key]-expected[key]) < 1e-12, (key, stats[key], expected[key])
        matches.append(key)
    assert np.allclose(stats['n1_range_m'], expected['n1_range_m'], atol=1e-12)
    for name, value in expected['correlation_with_future_n1'].items():
        assert abs(stats['correlation_with_future_n1'][name]-value) < 1e-10
    for name, distance in expected['T1_pipe_distances_m'].items():
        actual = topology['T1_to_measured_pressure_nodes'][name]['distance_m']
        assert round(actual) == distance, (name, actual, distance)
        assert abs(actual-expected['T1_pipe_distances_unrounded_m'][name]) < 1e-8
    assert counts['source_records'] == 47 and counts['groups'] == 35
    assert [r['groups'] for r in counts['categories']] == [27, 3, 2, 2, 1]
    assert topology['network_counts'] == expected['network_counts']
    assert topology['sectors']['C']['junctions'] == 92
    assert topology['sectors']['C']['pressure_sensors'] == ['n1', 'n4', 'n31']
    assert topology['sectors']['C']['AMR_count'] == 82
    checks = {'expected_numeric_claims_match': True, 'table_checks_pass': True,
              'reported_2018_correlations_match': True, 'reported_pipe_distances_match': True,
              'historical_inventory_counts_match': True, 'models_trained': False,
              '2019_use': 'Structural checks only; no predictive result computed.',
              'figure_reproduction': 'Same source values and plotting procedure; portable font, not a claim of pixel identity.',
              'human_independent_review': 'Not established by an automated run.'}
    dump(output / 'verification.json', checks)
    output_hashes = {str(path.relative_to(output)).replace('\\', '/'): digest(path)
                     for path in sorted(output.rglob('*')) if path.is_file() and path.name != 'run_manifest.json'}
    code_hashes = {str(path.relative_to(ROOT)).replace('\\', '/'): digest(path)
                   for path in sorted((ROOT / 'repro').glob('*.py'))}
    code_hashes['reproduce.py'] = digest(ROOT / 'reproduce.py')
    classification_inputs = {name: digest(ROOT / name) for name in [
        'literature/inventory_sources.csv', 'literature/group_assignments.csv',
        'data/source_manifest.json', 'provenance/expected_report_values.json']}
    manifest = {
        'run_utc': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
        'python': sys.version, 'platform': platform.platform(),
        'packages': {n: importlib.metadata.version(n) for n in ['numpy', 'pandas', 'matplotlib', 'PyYAML']},
        'inputs': inputs, 'code_sha256': code_hashes, 'classification_and_protocol_sha256': classification_inputs,
        'output_sha256': output_hashes,
        'results_status': 'Executed descriptive reproduction; no learned models trained.',
        'authorship': 'Project-specific code assembled/refactored with OpenAI Codex assistance; external methods/data credited separately.',
    }
    dump(output / 'run_manifest.json', manifest)
    print(json.dumps({'status': 'PASS', 'rows': stats['rows'], 'pairs': stats['forecast_pairs'],
                      'MAE_m': stats['persistence_MAE_m'], 'RMSE_m': stats['persistence_RMSE_m'],
                      'groups': counts['groups'], 'models_trained': False}, indent=2), flush=True)
    return stats, topology, counts


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--data-dir', type=Path, default=ROOT / 'data/raw')
    parser.add_argument('--output-dir', type=Path, default=ROOT / 'results/reproduced')
    parser.add_argument('--download', action='store_true', help='Download missing official files and verify their published MD5.')
    parser.add_argument('--import-data', type=Path, help='Optional flat cache of the exact official files; every checksum is checked.')
    args = parser.parse_args()
    run(args.data_dir, args.output_dir, args.download, args.import_data)

````

WRENG-7321\\n\\nJun 3, 2026\\n\\n# Dynamic Pressure Regulation in Water Distribution Systems through Remote Critical Node Control: A Case Study\\n\\nAuthors: A. Arango-Martinez https://orcid.org/0009-0003-9687-123X, E. Rosero-García https://orcid.org/0000-0003-2550-0207, and E. Franco-Mejia, Ph.D.Author Affiliations\\n\\nPublication: Journal of Water Resources Planning and Management\\n\\nVolume 152, Issue 8\\n\\n[Input]\\n\\n61\\n\\n### Metrics\\n\\n### Total Downloads 61\\n\\n  * Last 12 Months 61\\n\\nGet Access\\n\\n## Abstract\\n\\nThis paper presents a field-oriented methodology for modeling and controlling pressure regulation, demonstrated on a real water distribution network (WDN) sector. The aim is to provide a practical and reproducible pathway from field measurements to deployable real-time pressure control, addressing the gap between simulator-based numerical studies and full-scale field implementations. The study covers data-driven dynamic modeling, controller design, and pre-deployment performance assessment using real operational data. A low-order linear dynamic model with transport delay is identified directly from field measurements, combining 20 days of pressure and inflow data with step-response tests performed on an existing pressure-reducing valve (PRV). The model is validated against an independent 24-h dataset of real consumption, achieving a maximum deviation of approximately ±1  m H2⁢O at the remote critical node, which is adequate for control-oriented modeling and controller synthesis. Based on the identified model, a digital reference signal tracking (RST) controller with a Smith predictor is designed using explicit performance specifications for settling time, overshoot, and bandwidth. The controller is evaluated in silico using real consumption data not used during identification, providing an unbiased assessment of closed-loop performance under representative demand variability. Results show that the proposed strategy maintains pressure at the remote critical node within acceptable bounds while effectively rejecting demand-induced disturbances. Although closed-loop field implementation is beyond the scope of this study, the framework is explicitly conceived as a preparatory step for real-time deployment. The main conclusion is that a transparent, data-driven methodology grounded in field measurements can reduce modeling and tuning effort while yielding controller designs compatible with standard programmable logic controller (PLC) and supervisory control and data acquisition (SCADA) platforms, enabling utilities to progress systematically toward practical real-time pressure control.\\n\\n## Practical Applications\\n\\nThis study offers practical guidance for water utilities seeking to improve pressure management while reducing water losses and infrastructure wear. Using real operational data, the proposed approach shows how effective pressure regulation strategies can be derived directly from field measurements and implemented with standard PLC and SCADA systems already used by most utilities. As a result, the methodology is accessible without requiring advanced hydraulic-modeling expertise or specialized simulation tools. Pressure management is particularly important because daily demand variations often produce excessive pressures during low consumption periods (e.g., nighttime). These pressure surges increase the likelihood of pipe bursts, background leakage, and premature deterioration of network assets. The proposed control strategy addresses this issue by stabilizing pressure at a critical point in the network, allowing utilities to maintain pressures within acceptable and regulatory limits despite changing demand. While the controller has not yet been deployed in closed-loop field operation, the methodology is designed as a practical step toward real-time implementation. By reducing modeling effort and simplifying controller tuning, the approach lowers technical barriers for utilities interested in advanced pressure control and provides a realistic foundation for improving network reliability and reducing operational costs.\\n\\n\\n## Data Availability Statement\\n\\nSome or all data, models, or code that support the findings of this study are available from the corresponding author upon reasonable request.\\n\\n## Acknowledgments\\n\\nThe authors would like to thank Aquaoccidente S.A. # DS675 Mini-Project: reproducible BattLeDIM / L-Town evidence

````text
Public supporting material for **Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting**, NJIT DS675.
The proposed task is recorded pressure at **n1, 15 minutes ahead**. This release addresses
the provenance questions raised after Milestone 2. **It reproduces descriptive analyses;
it does not train or evaluate the proposed learning models.**

## Start here

| Need | Open |
|---|---|
| Which claim or figure is supported by which code? | [Claim-to-evidence ledger](docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE.md) |
| Who produced what, and with what assistance? | [Attribution and analysis status](docs/ATTRIBUTION.md) |
| Inspect the calculations step by step | [Executed explanatory notebook](notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb) |
| See the recomputed numerical results | [EDA statistics](results/reproduced/eda_statistics.json), [verification](results/reproduced/verification.json) |
| Inspect Figure 4's complete underlying inventory | [47 source records](literature/inventory_sources.csv), [35 group assignments](literature/group_assignments.csv) |
| Read the papers and their redistribution status | [Literature index](literature/README.md) |
| Understand and run the material step by step | [Reproduction guide](docs/REPRODUCTION_GUIDE.md) |

## Reproduce from a fresh clone

Python **3.14** is the tested local environment. The pinned scientific dependencies are in
`requirements.txt`. Raw data are downloaded from the fixed original Zenodo record;
no institutional login is needed for those data.

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Andres-ArangoMartinez/DS675_MiniProject.git
cd DS675_MiniProject
python -m venv .venv
```

Select the environment's Python. On Windows PowerShell:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe reproduce.py --download
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

On Linux/macOS, use `.venv/bin/python` in the same commands. Do not run the historical
source snapshots in `provenance/historical_scripts/`; they document the earlier work,
whereas `reproduce.py` is the portable entry point.

The first run downloads approximately 170 MB. Each of the 11 required files is verified
against its official MD5, then recorded with SHA-256. The CSVs are not duplicated in Git:
[data/source_manifest.json](data/source_manifest.json) gives the exact source URLs,
checksums, attribution and **CC-BY-4.0** license. A local flat cache can instead be supplied
with `--import-data /path/to/cache`. An altered file fails validation.

| Timestamps / complete days | 78,624 / 273 |
| Valid n1(+15) pairs | 78,621 |
| Persistence MAE | 0.05177624298851451 m |
| Persistence RMSE | 0.06849175638676988 m |
| n1 pressure range | 25.67-29.36 m |
| Nominal T1-to-n1 / n4 / n31 pipe distances | 891.3208 / 573.7565 / 307.7926 m |
| Reported rounded distances | 891 / 574 / 308 m |
| Historical literature inventory | 47 source records, 35 related-work groups |

These are **2018 descriptive results**, not final 2019 forecast scores. The pipeline reads
2019 only to check table structure, timestamps and missing values. It never fits a learned
model or computes a 2019 prediction score.

Outputs include the eight-table audit, correlations, sample forecast rows, full derived
tables (generated locally, ignored by Git), nominal pipe paths, regrouped literature counts,
Figures 2-4, package versions and input/code/output hashes. See
[results/reproduced/](results/reproduced/). The portable plots use DejaVu Serif; archived
report images retain their original bytes. Numerical reproduction does not imply pixel identity.

## Scope and limits

- This package was assembled and executed **with OpenAI Codex assistance**. It does not
  establish independent student implementation, understanding or review. See the attribution record.
- The 35-group inventory is a **historical, AI-assisted curated collection**, not an exhaustive
  systematic review. Recounting it does not repeat a historical search engine or validate every judgment.
- Ahmed et al. (2025) is a newly identified related study, **outside the historical 35-group count**.
  Its five signals and reconstruction objective must be considered in the next literature revision.
- Pipe distance is not pressure-propagation delay. A tank boundary does not prove upstream
  statistical independence. A fixed nominal PRV setting does not fix every remote pressure.
- Gradient Boosting, Ridge, Random Forest and the A/B/C feature comparisons remain **proposed**.
- The previous report is preserved as a historical artifact; this release does not silently
  rewrite the submitted report or claim that these public links existed at submission time.

## Sources and third-party rights

Data: Vrachimis et al., [Dataset of BattLeDIM, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.4017659](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.4017659).
Benchmark: [Vrachimis et al. (2022)](https://doi.org/10.1061/(ASCE)WR.1943-5452.0001601).
PDFs are included only where a document or source record supplies a redistribution license.
Other works have original DOI/publisher/author links in the literature catalog. Public access
or an institutional download alone is not treated as redistribution permission.
Third-party papers keep their own licenses; no repository-wide license is applied to them.
MILESTONE 2 ASSESSMENT

Description of Dataset and Learning Task: Very Good

Your description of the BattLeDIM dataset is thorough and technically informative. The supplied Zenodo link is valid, and the reported data volume, measurement types, and available variables are consistent with the dataset documentation.

Your proposed task is clearly defined as supervised regression: predicting pressure at n1 fifteen minutes ahead. The selected predictors and proposed construction of time-lagged features are appropriate. The dataset is sufficiently large for the proposed learning methods.

However, Section 1.3 explicitly presents substantial exploratory data analysis (EDA) as your own work, including numerical calculations, correlations, and figures. You also claim to have performed independent checks of the data tables and hydraulic network. No code or other reproducible record is provided to substantiate these claims.

Although performing EDA independently at this stage is permissible, it is not required for Milestone 2. If you choose to present it as original work, its provenance must be clear and verifiable.

Kaggle Contributions: Very Good

You provide a detailed and useful discussion of six relevant studies, including their learning tasks, data preparation, models, code references, evaluation measures, and reported results.

I appreciated your careful distinction between future-pressure forecasting, current-pressure reconstruction, and leak detection. These distinctions are important for determining which previous results are directly comparable with your proposed task.

Your claimed screening of 35 groups of studies is not accompanied by a complete inventory or a reproducible account of the screening process. Since Figure 4 is explicitly presented as your own analysis, you should provide supporting evidence or clarify the source of the information.

Your literature review should also consider Ahmed et al. (2025), "Windowed Multivariate LSTM Autoencoder for Leak Detection in Water Distribution Networks." This study uses the same five principal Area C measurements that you selected, although for a different learning task. Your proposed sixth signal and forecasting objective may still constitute meaningful extensions, but their relationship to existing work should be explained.

Your Anticipated Contributions: Problematic

The proposed supervised learning experiment is clearly specified, and the planned comparison of Gradient Boosting, Ridge, Random Forest, and persistence is technically feasible.

However, there is a serious unresolved issue concerning the originality of the analysis upon which your proposal is based. You repeatedly describe extensive computational results as your own, despite providing no code, notebooks, or other evidence showing how they were obtained.

This makes it difficult to determine which analyses you performed independently, which may have been reproduced or adapted from existing work, and which remain merely proposed. Consequently, the originality of the groundwork supporting your proposed contributions cannot presently be established.

This rating reflects the unresolved questions about attribution and provenance. It is not a determination that an Academic Integrity violation has occurred.

Your proposed contribution also needs a more convincing justification beyond applying established regression models to a narrowly specified forecasting task. Explain precisely what new understanding the experiments are intended to provide, and how the value of your proposed additional predictors will be demonstrated.

Presentation: Problematic

Your report is exceptionally well organized and visually polished. The explanations, tables, figures, and references are generally clear and concise.

However, professional technical reporting requires clear attribution and reproducibility, not merely polished presentation. Labeling figures and numerical results as "Own EDA" without identifying how they were produced is a significant reporting weakness.

The distinction between completed analysis, reproduced analysis, observations drawn from the literature, and proposed future work must be made explicit throughout the report.

Provide appropriate citations for externally obtained results and links to the code supporting any computational work that you claim to have performed.

ADVICE FOR THE NEXT STEPS IN YOUR PROJECT

First, please clarify the provenance of the EDA and other analyses presented as your own. If you performed these analyses independently, provide the corresponding code or notebooks, including the procedures used to generate Figures 2-4, calculate the reported correlations and persistence errors, examine the SCADA tables, and determine the hydraulic path distances.

If any of these results or figures were obtained from existing publications, repositories, tutorials, or other sources, identify those sources explicitly and revise the descriptions so that the authorship of each contribution is accurately represented.

Since Milestone 2 did not require implementation, you will not be penalized for work that has not yet been performed. It is important, however, not to represent anticipated work or externally obtained results as independently completed analysis.

For your final report, retain the useful discussion of previous models, but strengthen the explanation of how your contribution differs from existing studies. In particular, distinguish the novelty of the forecasting task, feature selection, experimental comparisons, and evaluation methodology.

Your reported persistence baseline already achieves a very small error. This suggests that improving on persistence may be difficult. Determine whether Gradient Boosting provides a meaningful improvement, and whether the additional signals improve prediction beyond what can be achieved using n1 history alone.

Ensure that all model comparisons use identical forecast targets, evaluation periods, and performance measures. Preserve chronological ordering, prevent future information from entering the predictors, and avoid selecting model settings using the final test period.

The proposed experiments are manageable for a four-week group project, provided that the group concentrates on reproducible data preparation, a small number of well-justified models, and carefully controlled comparisons.

Please proceed to Milestone 3 by advertising your proposal and forming a group. Before developing this proposal into the introductory material for your final report, however, the questions concerning the provenance and originality of the claimed analyses must be resolved.

- Michael Houle
````

```text
"""Execute the explanatory notebook with this environment's kernel, without a global install."""
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import json
import os
import sys
import nbformat
from nbclient import NotebookClient

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
kernel_root = Path(sys.prefix) / 'share/jupyter'
kernel_dir = kernel_root / 'kernels/ds675-repro'
kernel_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
(kernel_dir / 'kernel.json').write_text(json.dumps({
    'argv': [sys.executable, '-m', 'ipykernel_launcher', '-f', '{connection_file}'],
    'display_name': 'DS675 reproducibility', 'language': 'python',
}), encoding='utf-8')
os.environ['JUPYTER_PATH'] = str(kernel_root)
path = ROOT / 'notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb'
notebook = nbformat.read(path, as_version=4)
client = NotebookClient(notebook, timeout=180, kernel_name='ds675-repro',
                        resources={'metadata': {'path': str(ROOT)}})
client.execute()
nbformat.validate(notebook)
assert not any(output.output_type == 'error' for cell in notebook.cells if cell.cell_type == 'code' for output in cell.outputs)
nbformat.write(notebook, path)
record = {'executed_utc': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(), 'python_version': sys.version,
          'code_cells_executed': sum(cell.cell_type == 'code' for cell in notebook.cells),
          'cell_errors': 0, 'kernel_environment': 'Project virtual environment; no global kernelspec installed.',
          'models_trained': False, 'human_independent_review': 'Not established by this execution.'}
(ROOT / 'provenance/notebook_execution.json').write_text(json.dumps(record, indent=2)+'\n', encoding='utf-8')
print(json.dumps(record))

"""Acquire the fixed Zenodo release and validate its published checksums."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib
import json
import shutil
import time
import urllib.request

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]


def digest(path, algorithm='sha256'):
    h = hashlib.new(algorithm)
    with Path(path).open('rb') as handle:
        for block in iter(lambda: handle.read(1024 * 1024), b''):
            h.update(block)
    return h.hexdigest()


def source_manifest():
    return json.loads((ROOT / 'data/source_manifest.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))


def ensure_data(folder, download=False, import_folder=None):
    """Do not silently substitute a different mirror or dataset release."""
    folder = Path(folder)
    folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    manifest = source_manifest()
    records = []
    for source in manifest['files']:
        path = folder / source['filename']
        method = 'existing_local_file_checked_against_official_md5'
        if not path.exists() and import_folder is not None:
            candidate = Path(import_folder) / source['filename']
            if candidate.exists():
                shutil.copyfile(candidate, path)
                method = 'imported_local_cache_checked_against_official_md5'
        if not path.exists():
            if not download:
                raise FileNotFoundError(
                    f'{source["filename"]} is missing. Run python reproduce.py --download '
                    'or specify --import-data /path/to/flat/cache.'
                )
            print(f'Downloading {source["filename"]} from Zenodo ...', flush=True)
            temporary = path.with_name(path.name + '.part')
            for attempt in range(3):
                try:
                    request = urllib.request.Request(
                        source['download_url'], headers={'User-Agent': 'DS675-reproducibility/1.0'}
                    )
                    with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=45) as response, temporary.open('wb') as output:
                        shutil.copyfileobj(response, output)
                    if digest(temporary, 'md5') != source['md5']:
                        raise ValueError(f'Official checksum mismatch: {source["filename"]}')
                    temporary.replace(path)
                    break
                except Exception:
                    if temporary.exists():
                        temporary.unlink()
                    if attempt == 2:
                        raise
                    time.sleep(1)
            method = 'downloaded_from_official_zenodo_url'
        actual = digest(path, 'md5')
        if actual != source['md5']:
            raise ValueError(f'Checksum mismatch for {source["filename"]}: {actual}')
        records.append({
            'filename': path.name, 'acquisition': method, 'bytes': path.stat().st_size,
            'md5': actual, 'official_md5_matches': True, 'sha256': digest(path),
            'source_url': source['download_url'], 'license': 'CC-BY-4.0',
        })
        print(f'Verified {path.name}', flush=True)
    return records
```
